# FYP CA Report Audit: Handoff

**Written:** 2026-11-04, about 11:40–12:00 SGT, by the local Claude Code session (Opus 5.5). The owner paused the work so a Claude Code Cloud session can continue it.

**Hard deadline:** the CP4101 CA report is due **today, Wed 2026-11-04, 17:00 SGT**. It goes as a softcopy to the supervisor **and** the Main Evaluator.

**State in one line:** the 15-agent review of the CA report finished its research and verification (30 of 31 agents). The final synthesis agent failed on a usage limit, so no merged review exists yet. Nobody has edited the report because of the review.

**Goal for the next session (owner's words, 2026-11-04):** "Nên nhớ mục tiêu hiện tại của chúng ta là cho CA của FYP". The aim is the CA, so convey what is needed for it and move fast.

How this file was made:
- Sections 1–10 are hand-written from the full session transcript, the memory files and the panel results.
- The parts marked *(generated)* and Appendices A–C are copied from the agents' returned data by [`fyp-audit-artifacts/gen-handoff.js`](fyp-audit-artifacts/gen-handoff.js) and [`fyp-audit-artifacts/assemble.js`](fyp-audit-artifacts/assemble.js). They are not retyped.
- In generated text, every `|` became `/` and every line break a space, to keep the Markdown intact. The exact text is in [`fyp-audit-panel-results.json`](fyp-audit-panel-results.json).

## 1. Project Overview and Original Objectives

### 1.1 The project

- **VisuAlgo+** rebuilds VisuAlgo (visualgo.net, by A/P Steven Halim) on a modern foundation, with a redesigned interface, while keeping its educational purpose.
- **Student:** Truong Van Quoc Bao ("Bao"), A0276275U, GitHub `BJoozz`. The module is **NUS SoC CP4101** (B.Comp. Dissertation, 12 units), AY2026/27 Semester 1 (CA) and Semester 2 (final).
- **Bao's scope**, from his own project description of 2026-11-03, quoted in evidence doc 05 §1.1: the visualization and interaction layer. That covers the graph, tree and sorting visualizers, input editing and step-by-step playback, and integration into one consistent interface.
- **Kerk Tai Heng** is a fellow CP4101 student with the same supervisor. His scope is the engine/WASM foundation, the website design system, and shared infrastructure (web, cache, cloud). He also did the one-app consolidation on 2026-10-18. Integration with the WASM engine is future work.
- **Supervisor's early direction**, in Bao's words: the existing Laravel/PHP, MySQL, d3.js and jQuery stack should move to a modern foundation for both the interface and the execution engine.
- **The five project objectives**, from Bao's description, also on report p.2:
  1. Select suitable modern, widely adopted technologies.
  2. Preserve VisuAlgo's useful exploration, input editing and step-by-step explanation.
  3. Provide a consistent, redesigned interface across modules.
  4. Set clear boundaries for extending visualizers and integrating engines.
  5. Validate the rebuilt functionality and identify what still needs technical and user evaluation.

### 1.2 Objectives of this work (three stages in this session)

1. **Evidence pack** (asked 2026-11-01, done 2026-11-03). An audit of the project history, collecting traceable evidence for the CA report: five Markdown documents, figures and research notes in `BJoozz/docsforfyp`. The brief explicitly said **not** to write the report. Its full rules are in section 3.1.
2. **Compile the CA report to PDF** (asked 2026-11-04 09:40, done). Codex had written the LaTeX report and could not compile it.
3. **Review the CA report** (asked 2026-11-04 10:08, **paused at the synthesis step**). Bao asked in Vietnamese, translated: *"Run an overall re-check, cross-checking against the commits and files to see whether anything is inaccurate, and ask me if something needs confirming. Also tell me if something should be added or cut."* The review brief he pasted is reproduced verbatim:

> Review my **NUS SoC CP4101 FYP Continuous Assessment (CA) Report** critically, based on the official CA guidelines and evaluation criteria.
>
> This is a **Semester 1 progress report (10–15 pages), not the final FYP report**. Evaluate it against these key criteria:
>
> 1. **Problem Understanding (30%)**: Are the motivation, problem statement, objectives, scope, and limitations clearly explained?
> 2. **Technical Achievement (40%)**: Are the methodology, design decisions, implementation, individual contributions, and preliminary results technically convincing and supported by evidence?
> 3. **Literature Review**: Are relevant existing works discussed critically, with clear comparisons and justification for the chosen approach?
> 4. **Project Management (10–20%)**: Does the report demonstrate meaningful Semester 1 progress, initiative, challenges addressed, and a realistic Semester 2 plan?
> 5. **Report & Communication (10–20%)**: Is the report well-structured, concise, readable, properly referenced, and understandable to a Main Evaluator unfamiliar with the project?
> 6. **Accuracy & Evidence**: Are claims supported by actual implementations, experiments, screenshots, or references? Are completed work, ongoing work, future plans, and contributions from collaborators clearly distinguished?
>
> **Review instructions:**
> - Check both technical content and PDF presentation (formatting, figures, captions, readability, page count).
> - Identify missing information, unsupported claims, inconsistencies, repetition, and weak explanations.
> - Prioritize issues by severity: **Critical / Important / Minor**.
> - Provide actionable improvements with specific page or section references.
> - Assess how well the report prepares the reader for the CA Presentation and Q&A.
> - Give an overall readiness assessment and the **top 5 fixes before submission**.
>
> Be strict but fair. Do not expect a fully completed project or final experimental results. Distinguish official NUS requirements from recommendations, and do not invent missing evidence.

**Correction to the brief's weights.** The official CA forms put Project Management at 10% on the Main Evaluator's form only, and Report at 10% (supervisor) or 20% (Main Evaluator). See section 7.3.

### 1.3 Expected final deliverables (still owed)

1. **A review for Bao, in Vietnamese.** Technical terms, file names and quoted report text stay in English. Its parts, in order:
   1. Overall readiness: 5–8 sentences, with a one-line judgement per criterion, using the **official** weights.
   2. Top 5 fixes before submission.
   3. Deduplicated findings by severity (Critical / Important / Minor), ordered by page, each as "[page, §] issue → fix (basis)".
   4. A claim-check table: claim | page | source | MATCH / MISMATCH with the true value / UNVERIFIABLE. Include the matches.
   5. What to add and what to cut, with page references and lines saved.
   6. Questions for Bao, with options.
   7. CA presentation preparation: likely questions, slide outline and demo script.
   8. At most 10 strengths to keep.
2. **After Bao approves fixes:** a revised `report/main.tex`, with `report/vi/full.tex` and `report/vi/summary.tex` kept in sync. Then rebuilt PDFs at **≤ 15 pages** with **no "Draft" marker**.
3. **Optionally:** record Bao's trial answers in `report/week10_user_trial_addendum.md`.

### 2.5 2026-11-04 09:40–10:05: PDF compilation (done; uncommitted)

- **No TeX on the machine.** I started Docker Desktop and pulled `texlive/texlive:latest-medium`. Bao asked "sao ko tải thẳng biên dịch latex về luôn" (why not install LaTeX directly?). I installed **MiKTeX 25.12** with winget for the user, enabled automatic package installs, and installed the `dejavu` font package. I then stopped Docker. The Docker image (~2 GB) is still on the machine, unused. Direct install is the preferred approach.
- **Page-count regression found.** Codex's sources used 14 hard `\page`=`\clearpage` breaks tuned to the old text. After the Week 10 additions, the English PDF grew from 15 to **19 pages**, with three pages of 1–4 lines. The pre-update source compiles to 15 pages on MiKTeX too, so the toolchain is not the cause.
- **The fix.** `\page` is now `\par\penalty0 \ifdim\pagetotal>.72\textheight\clearpage\else\vspace{8pt}\fi`, in `main.tex` line 32 and `vi/style.tex`. The five `\clearpage` lines in `vi/full.tex` now use `\page`. `\penalty0` is required: without it, `\pagetotal` still counts an overflowing line and the macro breaks wrongly.
- **Result:** English **16 pages**, Vietnamese full 21, Vietnamese summary 4. There are 0 undefined references and 0 overfull boxes. The three PDFs were copied in, and both READMEs were updated.
- **None of this is committed** (8 modified files; see 4.3). Bao has not explicitly approved the macro change.

### 2.6 2026-11-04 10:08–11:30: the review panel

- **Inputs.** I made a page-annotated text of the 16-page PDF (now `fyp-audit-artifacts/report-snapshot/report-pages-16p.txt`) and 80-dpi page PNGs. The PNGs are local only and not preserved; rebuild them from the PDF.
- **The panel.** Workflow `ca-report-review`, run `wf_976de5d7-7c7`, ran 15 finders, each with one slice, and 15 adversarial verifiers, all Opus at max effort. It took about 51 minutes and 7.07 M subagent tokens.
- **The failure.** The synthesis agent failed: "You've hit your 5-hour limit · resets 2:40pm (Asia/Singapore)".
- **The pause.** Bao then paused everything, asked for this handoff, and switched the session model to Opus 5.5.

### 2.7 Insights worth keeping (from all stages)

- **The Week 10 chronology.** The Registrar puts Week 10 at **19–23 Oct 2026**. The only dated record of the floating-text decision is Bao's request of **23 Oct** (TP-34 in `prototypes/docs/tree/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md`; PR #29 opened 13:36 SGT), and it mentions no trial. Floating *number* popups already existed (`ff77eca`, 13 Oct, TP-20). "Following the Week 10 trial" (p.11) is therefore true only if every session came before the 23 Oct request.
- **The cited evidence contradicts the trial.** The pinned snapshot `67ce56c` (refs [6]–[8]) says **no user evaluation exists**. A reader who follows the citation on the trial sentence finds the opposite claim.
- **The V2/V7 survey.** The report says Kerk's survey informed "the choice between V2 and V7". Bao's recorded answer says the survey came first, then Kerk and Bao picked V2 and V7 from the **nine** layouts in **Kerk's wireframe** (`69aa8e4`), and both were built by Bao.
- **Evidence references.** `BJoozz/docsforfyp` is **private** (`isPrivate=true`; only BJoozz is a collaborator), so examiners following [6]–[8] get a 404. A public build exists at https://kerktaiheng.github.io/visualgo-plus-design/ (`official-added-3`).
- **The 2026-11-01 test run** recorded 3,273 unit tests (654 graph node:test plus 2,619 vitest) and 151 browser cases, all passing. On 2026-11-04 the `counts` agent re-ran graph (654/654), vitest (2,619/2,619) and lint (0 errors, 2 warnings) live. Playwright was not re-run.

## 3. User Requirements, Preferences, and Decisions

### 3.1 Rules from the 2026-11-01 evidence-pack brief (still in force for audit work)

- **Repository changes:** "Do not modify application code, switch the working branch, reset files, merge, commit, or push. Preserve uncommitted work."
- **Access limits:** state any limit on access or history; never claim to have reviewed what was inaccessible.
- **Labels:** VERIFIED / DOCUMENTED / INFERENCE / QUESTION.
- **Tests:** a test file proves a test exists, not that it passes. Keep historical results separate from tests run during the audit.
- **Claims:** do not invent performance gains, user feedback, usability or educational benefits, or novelty. If there is no benchmark or user study, say so.
- **Prototypes:** do not present prototypes as the current product.
- **Ownership:** do not infer it from a filename, commit count or merge commit.

### 3.2 Standing preferences (memory files and repeated instructions)

- **Language:** write to Bao in **Vietnamese**; commits, PRs and docs in **English**.
- **Questions:** ask with **concrete explanations, links and options** (2026-11-03). Ask only when a choice is truly his.
- **Review findings:** verify each finding before fixing it, explain the current rules, offer fix options, then let Bao choose. This applies to pasted Codex reviews and to this panel.
- **Codex output:** Bao does **not** read Codex's plans or reviews. A pasted one is input, never his agreement.
- **AI-assisted work is Bao's own** (2026-11-03). Say it once; build no attribution scaffolding.
- **Rule changes:** DD/TP/SP records, AGENTS.md rules and test invariants change only on Bao's word.
- **Commits:** by explicit paths only; never from `git status`, since Codex and other sessions write into the same worktrees. Push only when told. Bao merges PRs himself.
- **Agents:** every agent runs on **Opus at max effort, never Fable** (since 2026-10-21). Keep the fan-out small, and test only what a change can affect.
- **Dev servers:** never touch one you did not start; port 5186 may be Bao's own. Stop your own by port or PID after checking ownership. Pass Playwright `--output` outside OneDrive.
- **Docs:** describe the current state; no stage logs.
- **Priority now:** the CA. Focus, speed, and only the needed information (2026-11-04).

### 3.4 Decisions made in this session, with reasons

- **Evidence pack, not the report.** Codex wrote the report; this session audited and compiled it.
- **Toolchain.** MiKTeX installed directly instead of Docker, at Bao's request ("sao ko tải thẳng").
- **The `\page` macro.** It became conditional instead of a hard break, to remove nearly empty pages without changing any text. **This still needs Bao's approval** and is uncommitted.
- **The review panel design.** One finder per slice plus one adversarial verifier each gives independent checking. The synthesis was to be a single agent.
- **Handoff placement.** It lives in `docsforfyp/docs/`, the repo that holds the report, not in the app repo. The app repo is Kerk's shared repo, and the audit rules keep it untouched.

### 4.3 The CA report (`docsforfyp/report/`) as reviewed

- **`main.tex`** is the English report, built with pdfLaTeX into `VisuAlgo_CA_Report_Draft.pdf`. It has **16 pages**: cover p1, body p2–15 (references on p15), and two meta notes on p16.

  > VERDICT (Literature Review): partially meets the criterion. Section 2 is accurate and honest: every external claim checks out against its source, and the correctness-vs-learning boundary is well drawn. But it is thin and descriptive: three peer-reviewed works, the newest from 2012, and no comparison with any system other than an OpenDSA web page. §2.3 disclaims novelty instead of positioning the work. It also leaves unused the most useful content of its own sources: Hundhausen's activity list and measurement findings, Shaffer's quality and coverage findings and his Algorithms in Action precedent, and Halim 2012's future-work agenda and student feedback. For a CA report this is a fixable weakness, not a failure. LR-2, LR-3, LR-4, LR-7 and LR-8 are text swaps inside §2 and the reference list, at the same length, doable today. LR-5 and LR-6 need the owner's answers.
  > 
  > PROPOSED §2 STRUCTURE (about 470 words, the same as now):
  > - 2.1 VisuAlgo and comparable systems (~150 words): Halim 2012's four advantages, Halim 2015's Online Quiz, what VisuAlgo+ keeps and defers (LR-3), Galles DSV, OpenDSA/JSAV (one sentence), Algorithm Visualizer (one sentence, linked to the engine boundary in §7.1).
  > - 2.2 What makes algorithm visualization effective (~170 words): Hundhausen (activities, viewing alone, procedural measures), the Naps taxonomy, Saraiya (pace, examples, pseudocode and stepping back), Shaffer (quality, topic skew, lost AVs), each with its consequence for VisuAlgo+ (LR-4, LR-7).
  > - 2.3 Position of this work (~150 words): the comparator framed by Halim 2012's own agenda; what VisuAlgo+ concentrates on; layout (VS Code) and notes (split attention) precedents in one clause each; the three design questions tied to sources (LR-2, LR-6).
  > - Removed to make room: the React Flow/D3 paragraph (already in §4.1 and Figure 1), both Week 10 sentences (already in §6.1), and the 'does not establish/prove' disclaimers (keep one boundary sentence). This also removes the stray words at the top of p.4.

### 12. `crit-management`

- **Assigned task:** Criterion review: Project Management (claimed 10-20%) - Semester 1 progress, initiative, challenges, realism of the Semester 2 plan, risks.
- **Finder:** completed; 9 findings (1 Critical, 6 Important, 2 Minor); 7 positives
- **Verifier:** completed; 8 CONFIRMED, 1 REFUTED; 2 added
- **Unresolved (owner or further check):** none
- **Finder notes (verbatim):**

  > VERDICT (Project Management): The report clearly shows meaningful, well-evidenced Semester 1 progress. The management story around that progress is under-presented, and the Semester 2 plan is not yet realistic as written. Main gaps: (1) Table 5 schedules the final-report writing after the published deadline (Wed 7 Apr 2027) and leaves out the CA presentation and the exams; (2) the Week 10 trial is a vague claim with no dates, count or concrete observation; (3) no challenge is described as a problem overcome, and no slip is reflected on, although the repository has good examples; (4) Table 1 cannot be mapped to NUS weeks; (5) dependencies on Kerk, the risks and the ethics step are generic or missing. Suggested order before 17:00: PM-1, PM-2 (needs Bao's numbers), PM-4, PM-3, then PM-5, PM-6 and PM-7 as short additions. Estimated effort 1-1.5 hours. Page budget: the SoC CA guidelines say 10-15 pages (VERIFIED, FYP_CA_Report_Guidlines_Oct_22.pdf). The PDF has 16 pages including the title page and a back-matter page, and p.13 has about a third of a page free, so additions should be offset, e.g. by shortening 6.2's first paragraph and the 4.6 parallel-edge paragraph.
  > COULD NOT CHECK: The criteria tables on the CA evaluation form (comp.nus.edu.sg/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/CA-Evaluation-Form.pdf) are images. The text layer shows only that each form 'counts 15% towards the final grade', plus the feedback headings 'Work done and amount of effort invested so far', 'Areas of weakness' and 'What is expected at the end of the project'. The claimed 10-20% weight for project management is therefore unverified. I also could not check: the Week 10 recordings, participant count, dates and consent; Kerk's survey data; the current NUS-IRB and SoC rules for FYP user studies; and whether the supervisor has seen the plan. I read the SoC FYP page through WebFetch summaries (two fetches, consistent with each other). I read the calendar from the registrar's own PDF via pdftotext.
  > SIDE OBSERVATIONS FOR OTHER REVIEWERS: (a) The registrar calendar confirms that Week 1 began on Mon 10 Aug 2026, which closes evidence-pack Q1. (b) p.4 says Kerk's survey informed 'the choice between the V2 split-rail and V7 dock-based layouts', whereas the owner's Q8 answer (doc 01 line 25) says V2 and V7 were chosen after the survey. Choosing between the two is not the same as choosing the two out of nine, so confirm the wording with the owner. (c) 'DRAFT FOR REVIEW' (main.tex:48) and the 'Draft' running header (main.tex:27) are still in the PDF; remove them before submission (report/README.md:38). I edited no file in either repository.

  > 6. Algorithms and correctness (Figures 5 and 6; p.9-10): six graph algorithms added; the parallel-edge bridge pitfall; brute-force, Floyd-Warshall and exhaustive oracles; AVL Delete against a textbook reference; the sorting-comparison caveat (Figure 7).
  >    LIVE DEMO here (section C).
  > 7. Floating text and the Week 10 trial (Figure 8; p.11, p.13): the dead-step problem; who took part and what was seen; one shared note layer (Dijkstra, LCA, BST, AVL, Heap); placement and timing (1.6 s; no note when a step lasts under 200 ms); effect not yet measured.
  > 8. Status, limits and plan (Table 3, Table 5; p.12-15): what the tests do and do not show; limits (eager timelines, unbounded sorting history, desktop Chromium only, no accessibility audit, no coverage figure); the November-April plan; the engine slice in January; follow-up tasks (Table 4); lessons learned.
  > Backup slides: B1 Kerk's survey; B2 Table 3 breakdown and property tests; B3 the engine study (M0-M4) and the proto Edge {u, v}; B4 the sorting buffer (2,048 operations per pane) and the 60,000-operation gate (SP-06); B5 scale facts (section D).
  > 
  > C. LIVE DEMO: 'Dijkstra on the default /sssp graph', about 4 minutes. It shows a dead step and an update explained on the canvas, a drag that keeps the run, an edit that ends it, and a panel move that persists. Rehearse it once.
  > Setup: run `npm run dev` from the repository root (http://127.0.0.1:5186), or use the Pages build of 662e7b3 (https://kerktaiheng.github.io/visualgo-plus-design/). Open a fresh guest window, so no saved layout is restored, at about 1440x900, with an English locale and network access (needed for the Inter font). Keep a screen recording of the same steps as a fallback.
  > 1. On Home, click 'Single-Source Shortest Paths' (/sssp; Dijkstra is preselected, start node A). Point out the V10 default: Action and e-Lecture on the left, Pseudocode above Status on the right, the bottom empty.
  > 2. Press the footer's Next once; a paused run starts on Frame 1. Keep pressing Next until Frame 22/31. B -> D is rejected: a red 'already settled' note appears over D, and Status says 'Keep D unchanged because it is already settled.' The message: nothing moves, and the note says why (§4.8). A note lasts about 1.6 s and appears only on a forward step; press Previous then Next to replay it. (Frames per e2e/dijkstra.spec.ts:154-160.)
  > 3. Press Next twice to reach Frame 24/31. B -> E is accepted: a green '4 + 3 < 8 · update' note, E's badge changes from 8 to 7, Pseudocode line 7 lights up, and Status gives the same reason (Figure 8).
  > 4. Drag vertex E about 60 px. The run stays on Frame 24 and the note follows E: layout is not algorithm input (Table 2; e2e/dijkstra.spec.ts:248-276; Live force layout is off by default).
  > 5. Open Action -> 'Draw graph' and change the edge line 'B E 3' to 'B E 5'. The run disappears and the footer shows 'No run': a semantic edit invalidates the run (App.tsx:210-214; reconcileGraphText.ts:300-312). Press Next and step to the B -> E relaxation; by inference this is Frame 24 again, because the settle order is unchanged. Expect a red '4 + 5 > 8 · keep', the 'existing distance already smaller' case from §4.8 (inferred from dijkstraStepNotes.ts:39-43; not run).
  > 6. Press and drag the STATUS tab. Show the drop preview over the Pseudocode group (Figure 4), then drop it on the dashed band along the bottom of the drawing, the empty bottom region. Drag the new splitter; the run stays on its frame (DD-50). Reload the page: the layout persists in versioned local storage (p.7). Settings -> 'Reset panel layout' restores the default. Mention that the model's invariants are tested with 4,000 random operations, but the drag itself is checked by hand (p.7-8).
  > 7. Optional, 30 s: open /dfsbfs/cut-vertex-bridge, choose Select preset 'CP4 4.7', press Next, then Last. The result is cut vertices 1, 3, 4 and bridges 0-1, 3-4 (Figure 5); explain the parallel-edge rule. Untested extension: draw nodes A B C with edges 'A B', 'A B', 'B C'; only B-C should be a bridge.
  > Recovery: if a note is missed, press Previous then Next; if the layout gets messy, use Settings -> Reset panel layout; if the fonts or network fail, play the recording.
  > 
  > D. BACKUP FACTS (checked)
  > - Eager timeline size, from one dated observation: Bellman-Ford on 40 vertices and 160 undirected edges built 25,684 frames in 139 ms, retaining 32.05 MiB. This was on 2026-10-03, on an earlier build before DD-44, and is recorded as 'observations, not guarantees' (prototypes/docs/graph/BELLMAN_FORD_ACCEPTANCE.md:88-97).
  > - Largest bundled graph samples: 48, 62 and 63 vertices; MST Large has 54 vertices and 117 edges (prototypes/src/graph/algorithms/algorithmSamples.ts:187-315).
  > - Sorting: a future buffer of 2,048 operations per pane (operationBuffer.ts:7); an eager gate of 60,000 operations summed across panes, because operation counts cannot be predicted in advance (operationBuffer.ts:246; SP-06).
  > - Engine: Vertex { uint32 id } and Edge { uint32 u, v } with no edge id (wasm-arch/proto/graph/v1/graph.proto:25-52); 'The engine owns history' (wasm-arch/docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:5-18).
  > - Target windows: about 1366x657, 1280x600, 1536x730, 1440x789 and 1920x960 (prototypes/docs/graph/AGENTS.md:36-43).
  > - PRs: 37 (36 merged, #5 closed); Bao opened 33, and Kerk opened #18-#21, all on 18 Oct (gh).
  > - e-Lectures: graph 11 decks (40 slides), tree 7 (111), sorting 1 (9), written after visualgo.net's lectures (doc 05 §1.4; TP-14).
  > - Why Graph V1 was rewritten as V2: one large App, Edit/Run modes and two canvas branches (graph-visualizer-v2 DD-01, DD-02).
  > 
  > E. NOT CHECKED
  > - I did not run the app, the tests or any demo step (hard rule). The frame numbers 22 and 24 come from e2e/dijkstra.spec.ts, which passed on 2026-11-01 (doc 04 §2.1). The 'keep' note in demo step 5 is inferred; rehearse it.
  > - The Week 10 facts (participant count, dates, build, test, consent) and Kerk's survey data are not in any source I could read.
  > - NUS calendar: the registrar PDF returned 403, and I could not extract the SCALE PDF's text. 'Week 10 = 19-23 Oct 2026' is an INFERENCE from search snippets (teaching from Mon 10 Aug 2026; recess 19-27 Sep 2026).
  > - I did not check whether the Right-arrow shortcut starts a run from 'No run' (keyboardShortcuts.ts:80-81 maps it to 'next'), so press the footer's Next once first.
  > - I did not check the CP4101 CA presentation length or rubric.
  > - I viewed page layout only on PNGs 7, 8, 11 and 12 and the full-size F1 and F4 figures.
  > 
  > Web sources (calendar): [NUS Registrar AY2026/27 calendar (403)](https://nus.edu.sg/registrar/docs/info/calendar/ay2026-2027.pdf); [NUS SCALE AY2026/27 calendar](https://scale.nus.edu.sg/docs/default-source/btech-engineering/acadcalendar_btech-bit-spc_ay2026-27.pdf); [NUS Law AY2026/27 Sem 1 weekly calendar](https://law.nus.edu.sg/ay2026-27-law-weekly-calendar-sem-1-acad-ver-20-july-2026/); [acadcalendar.com NUS 2026-27](https://acadcalendar.com/nus-academic-calendar/).

## 6. Verified Findings and Supporting Evidence

### 6.1 Consolidated picture (hand-grouped from the confirmed findings; **preliminary, not a verified synthesis**)

I grouped the findings from their IDs and first sentences, with no new checking. The full text and evidence for each is in Appendix A.

**Critical.**
- **The PDF still says "draft" in four places:**
  - "DRAFT FOR REVIEW" on the cover (`main.tex` line 48).
  - The header "CP4101 CA Report | Draft" on pp. 2–16 (line 27).
  - The PDF title metadata "VisuAlgo+ — CA Report Draft" (line 13).
  - The file name `VisuAlgo_CA_Report_Draft.pdf`.
  - Findings: `official-OFF-1`, `crit-report-QR-1`.

**Factual inaccuracies:** the report states something the code, git or the cited source contradicts.

| Claim in the report | What the panel found | IDs |
|---|---|---|
| p.11: notes "are suppressed for intervals below 200 milliseconds"; duration "limited by the step interval and configured delay" | Misstates the code. The 200 ms floor applies to the note's remaining life, which is 90% of the step interval minus the delay. | `floating-FT-4`, `counts-NUM-1`, `crit-accuracy-ACC-18` |
| p.10, Figure 6: "the new value 1 moves above 7" | The frame (3 of 9) shows the comparison **before** the swap; 1 is still below 7. | `counts-NUM-4`, `algorithms-ALGFIG-3`, `floating-added-2` |
| p.12, Figure 9: "presents the rebuilt modules alongside clearly marked future topics" | The 1440×900 capture shows no "Coming soon" card. | `algorithms-ALGFIG-1`, `counts-NUM-5` |
| p.12: "Of the 30 catalogue cards, 12 open a module; eight are labelled full and four partial" | The counts are right, but they mix VisuAlgo+'s two editor cards with visualgo.net modules. "Full" is undefined, and 4 of the 8 "full" cards are not visualgo.net modules. | `counts-added-2`, `crit-accuracy-ACC-6`, `qa-QA-6`, `crit-problem-added-2` |
| p.7: "the drawing has a target floor of 360 by 320 pixels" (as if shared) | Graph only; tree and sorting pass their own floors. | `counts-NUM-3`, `architecture-ARCH-3` |
| p.5, Figure 1: rendering drawn as passing through the shared layer | The modules render directly. | `architecture-ARCH-1` |
| p.7, Figure 2: bottom region drawn full width | The left and right columns run full height; the bottom sits under the canvas. | `architecture-ARCH-2` |
| p.6 and p.13: sorting history "remains unbounded" | Never trimmed, but bounded: input is capped at 240 values. | `architecture-ARCH-4` |
| p.9: "700 graph inputs for cut-vertex/bridge checks" right after the oracle sentence | Only seeds 1–400 are oracle-compared. | `counts-NUM-2`, `algorithms-ALGFIG-2` |
| p.9: "End node where applicable" | Only BFS, Dijkstra and Bellman–Ford take one. | `algorithms-ALGFIG-6`, `crit-accuracy-ACC-8` |
| p.4: "Later choices 27–48 and the subsequent 'V10 wins' changes were joint decisions" | "V10 wins" (18 Oct) came after choices 27–32 and before 33–48. It names the one-app consolidation, which the next sentence credits to Kerk. | `timeline-added-1`, `crit-accuracy-ACC-9`, `contributions-CONTRIB-4` |
| p.4: "Kerk conducted a user survey to inform the choice between the V2 split-rail and V7 dock-based layouts" | Recorded answer: survey first, then Kerk and Bao picked V2/V7 from **nine** layouts in **Kerk's wireframe**, which is never credited. | `timeline-TL-3`, `contributions-CONTRIB-1`, `contributions-CONTRIB-2`, `crit-accuracy-ACC-4`, `qa-QA-3`, `crit-problem-added-1`, `crit-management-added-1` |
| p.4: "We also implemented both variants" | The V2 and V7 builds were Bao's; the sentence undersells him. | `crit-technical-TA-4` |
| p.4: two different "V2"s; "V10" never defined | Ambiguous to an outsider. | `contributions-added-2`, `qa-QA-7` |
| p.4, Table 1 periods | Graph extensions started in late September; pre-semester V1 work is merged into "Late July–August". | `timeline-TL-4`, `timeline-added-2` |
| p.11: "Following the Week 10 trial, I introduced floating text … [8, 6]" | The cited snapshot says there is no user evaluation. The dated record gives Bao's request of 23 Oct with no trial (see 2.7). | `timeline-TL-1`, `timeline-TL-2`, `floating-FT-2`, `literature-LIT-6`, `crit-accuracy-ACC-2`, `crit-accuracy-ACC-14`; uncertain: `floating-FT-1`, `crit-accuracy-ACC-1` |
| p.11: notes sit "beside the relevant node or edge" | Overstates the anchoring. | `floating-FT-5` |
| p.11: rejected relaxation as the example, Figure 8 accepted | The text never says rejected relaxations get notes. | `floating-FT-7` |
| p.3: "Halim et al. [2] present VisuAlgo" | The 2012 paper presents the predecessor and never uses the name; Halim 2015 calls VisuAlgo its continuation. | `literature-LIT-4`, `crit-literature-LR-3` |
| p.15, ref [4] | The OpenDSA URL fails with a TLS error. | `literature-LIT-5` |
| p.12: "266 CI runs" | Sits among the 1 November results, but the runs span 4 workflows and the whole history. | `crit-accuracy-ACC-11` |
| p.13: "developer checks … lack a complete recorded protocol" | The protocol exists (checklists of 20, 10 and 26 items); only the runs went unrecorded. | `crit-accuracy-ACC-17` |
| p.2, p.11, p.16: supervisor statements | They rest only on Bao's later account. | `crit-accuracy-ACC-5`; uncertain: `contributions-CONTRIB-3` |

**Important themes** (score-relevant, not factual errors):

- **Week 10 trial underspecified, and ethics not mentioned.**
  - There are no dates, participant count, tested build, test content or observations.
  - Video was recorded, but there is no word on consent or storage.
  - The planned 6–10-student study has no ethics or consent step.
  - IDs: `crit-management-PM-2`, `qa-QA-1`, `crit-management-PM-6`, `qa-QA-2`, `official-added-1`; uncertain: `crit-accuracy-ACC-15`.
- **Private evidence references [6]–[8]** are the most-cited sources and look like publications. IDs: `counts-added-1`, `floating-added-1`, `literature-LIT-1`, `crit-literature-LR-5`, `crit-report-QR-8`, `qa-QA-5`, `crit-accuracy-added-1`, `official-added-3`.
- **16 pages against the official "10-15".**
  - Dropping p.16 alone gives 15 pages (variant v1).
  - Prose de-duplication plus flexible floats gives 14 (v3/v4).
  - Cropped screenshots give 15 (v6).
  - IDs: `crit-report-QR-2`, `official-OFF-2`; cut list in the `crit-report` notes (5.1).
- **No explicit problem statement; objectives not measurable or traced.** Objective 1 has no selection rationale. IDs: `crit-problem-PU-1`, `crit-problem-PU-2`, `crit-problem-PU-3`, `official-OFF-7`, `contributions-CONTRIB-5`.
- **Literature review is thin, dated and descriptive.**
  - The newest peer-reviewed work is from 2012, and the work is not positioned against other systems.
  - VS Code is not credited as V10's precedent, and visualgo.net is not cited.
  - The literature counts under Understanding (30%).
  - IDs: `literature-LIT-2/3/7/8/9`, `literature-added-1..4`, `crit-literature-LR-1..6`, `crit-problem-PU-8`, `official-OFF-5`.
- **Technical contribution not stated crisply.** Decisions are given as WHAT with little WHY, although the 95 decision records have the reasons. Validation is not tied to the risks it addresses, and the scale of the work is undersold. IDs: `crit-technical-TA-1..4`, `qa-QA-9`.
- **Project management.** Table 5 ignores the 7 Apr 2027 final-report deadline. Challenges are not presented as real problems. Process evidence is invisible to the Main Evaluator. IDs: `crit-management-PM-1`, `official-OFF-3`, `crit-management-PM-3`, `official-OFF-6`.
- **Presentation:**
  - Caveats are repeated 13 times (`crit-report-QR-3`).
  - Screenshot text prints at about 3–4 pt (`QR-4`).
  - Most figures and tables are never referenced in the text (`QR-5`, `algorithms-added-1`).
  - Abbreviations are left unexpanded (`QR-6`).
  - Evidence-bookkeeping sentences sit in the body (`QR-7`, `official-added-2`, `crit-accuracy-ACC-3`).
  - One feature goes by four names (`floating-FT-3`, `crit-accuracy-ACC-13`).

**Candidate top 5 (my preliminary ranking; the next session should confirm or adjust it):**

1. Remove every "draft" marker.
2. Fix the Week 10 and floating-text passage: date it, cite the addendum instead of [8, 6], make the chronology fit 23 Oct, and add consent and ethics. This needs Bao's answers.
3. Correct the factual inaccuracies above.
4. Make the evidence reachable and reach ≤ 15 pages.
5. Add a problem statement and measurable objectives, and cut the repeated caveats.

### 7.2 Files preserved with this handoff (`docsforfyp/docs/`)

- **`FYP_AUDIT_HANDOFF.md`:** this file.
- **`fyp-audit-panel-results.json`:** all raw panel results. The `tasks` key holds the slice descriptions. `finder.<slice>` holds findings, positives and notes. `verifier.<slice>` holds verdicts and the added findings. `derived.{confirmed, uncertain, refuted, positives}` holds the merged lists, sorted. `failed` lists the failed agent.
- **`fyp-audit-artifacts/official/`:**
  - The CA guideline (PDF and text) and the CA evaluation form (text, extract PDF, and page images `CA-Evaluation-Form-2026-02-pages/form-1..9.png`).
  - The Registrar calendar (PDF and text), the text of the SoC FYP pages, and `nusmods-cp4101.json`.
- **`fyp-audit-artifacts/cut-list/`:**
  - `variants.py` builds the variants: `python variants.py <report_dir> <out_root>`.
  - `variants/*.tex` holds the six compiled variants; `PAGES.txt` gives v1 15, v2 15, v3 14, v4 14, v5 15 and v6 15 pages.
  - Helper scripts: `pngrows.py`, `inkrows.py` and `hedges.py`.
  - The variants were built from the 16-page source and are **proposals only**.
- **`fyp-audit-artifacts/factcheck/`:** the live test logs of 11-04 (`graph-test.log`, `vitest.log`, `lint.log`), the CI run list `runs.json`, and two counting scripts.
- **`fyp-audit-artifacts/report-snapshot/report-pages-16p.txt`:** the reviewed PDF as text with page markers. All panel page numbers refer to it.
- **Generator files:** `gen-handoff.js`, `assemble.js`, `head.md` and `mid.md` (the first handoff version's hand-written parts).
- **Not copied:** downloaded papers (copyrighted; URLs are in the findings), the 640 KB CI log, the 912 KB vitest JSON, the page PNGs and the variant PDFs. All can be regenerated.

Softcopy to the supervisor **and** the Main Evaluator by Wednesday of Week 12. It does not say whether the cover or references count. It sets no format or AI rule. |

The literature review counts under Understanding. Technical Achievement starts with "Formulation and problem statement". A or A+ only with justification. The form text confirms the weights (lines 24, 41, 62, 72, 139, 158, 177, 193). |

### 7.4 Building the PDFs

- **Bao's machine:** MiKTeX 25.12, at `C:\Users\123ba\AppData\Local\Programs\MiKTeX\miktex\bin\x64`, on the user PATH.
- **Cloud:** TeX Live with `latexmk`, `xelatex`, DejaVu fonts (`fonts-dejavu` or `texlive-fonts-extra`), and the packages used by `main.tex` and `vi/style.tex` (geometry, booktabs, tabularx, enumitem, float, xcolor, tikz, caption, fancyhdr, titlesec, hyperref, microtype, lmodern, needspace, placeins, fontspec).
- **Build outside OneDrive** (copy `report/` to a temp folder), then copy the PDFs back:

```sh
cd report && latexmk -pdf -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error main.tex     # → main.pdf; rename for submission (drop "Draft")
cd report/vi && latexmk -xelatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error -jobname=VisuAlgo_CA_Report_VI full.tex
cd report/vi && latexmk -xelatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error -jobname=VisuAlgo_CA_Summary_VI summary.tex
```

- **After building, check:**
  - The page count, with `pdfinfo`.
  - The log for `undefined` and `Overfull`.
  - `pdftotext` output, grepped for "Draft".
  - Page renders (`pdftoppm -r 60`), to spot nearly empty pages.

#### `crit-technical-TA-7` (Minor, finder said Important; UNCERTAIN)

- **Where:** page 9; §4.6, paragraph 'A representative correctness issue: parallel edges'; category undersold-correctness
- **Issue:** p.9 presents the parallel-edge rule as a hypothetical ('Ignoring both edges during DFS can incorrectly mark one as a bridge'). It never says that this modelling choice makes VisuAlgo+ answer differently from visualgo.net on two of visualgo's own samples. When a directed sample is read undirected, VisuAlgo+ keeps its two opposite arcs as two parallel edges; visualgo.net merges them into one edge, which is then a bridge. On CP4 4.22, VisuAlgo+ reports three bridges where visualgo.net reports four; on 'Large, Cycles', 24 where it reports 25. The supervisor knows VisuAlgo's answers and may read the difference as a defect unless the report explains it. Explained, it is strong evidence of algorithmic care.
- **Evidence (DOCUMENTED):** In code (VERIFIED): prototypes/src/graph/algorithms/algorithmSamples.ts:503-523. The comment says 'visualgo.net merges each pair into one edge, which is then a bridge, so those two examples answer otherwise there'. The CP4 4.22 description reads '1 — 2 is none, its arcs both ways two parallel edges here, where visualgo.net merges them into one edge, a fourth bridge'. The Large, Cycles description reads '24 bridges; 37 — 38 is none… a 25th bridge'. createCutVertexBridgeSequence.ts:169-170 skips the parent edge by id. cutVertexBridgeProperty.test.ts:37-39 generates self-loops and parallel copies, and :120 requires more than 100 graphs with a second edge to a parent. DOCUMENTED: prototypes/docs/graph/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md:2389-2390 (DD-60: visualgo.net 'skips every arc back to the parent vertex, which would call a doubled edge to the parent a bridge, a bug not copied') and the commit message of 0fc105c. I did not check visualgo.net's behaviour myself; a code search of BJoozz/visualgo returned no results.
- **Proposed fix:** If the owner agrees, rewrite the p.9 paragraph as proposed, framed strictly as a modelling difference: - VisuAlgo+ keeps a directed sample's opposite arcs as two parallel edges and skips the parent edge by id; visualgo.net treats them as one edge. - Name CP4 4.22 (3 vs 4 bridges) and Large, Cycles (24 vs 25). - Mention the test's more than 100 doubled-parent graphs. Otherwise leave the paragraph as it is.
- **Owner must confirm:** Have you confirmed on visualgo.net that CP4 4.22 and 'Large, Cycles' give four and 25 bridges there? And do you want the report to call it a modelling difference (merged arcs vs parallel edges) rather than a bug in the original?
- **Verifier:** The VisuAlgo+ side is verified (algorithmSamples.ts:504-523; createCutVertexBridgeSequence.ts:169-170). I also read visualgo's source (BJoozz/visualgo resources/views/viz/dfsbfs.blade.php, via gh api). Its DFS skips every arc back to the parent vertex (line 1162, 'else if (vertexB != p[u])'). It converts a directed graph by adding bidirectional edges (1047-1048), so opposite arcs act as one edge. This matches the code comment, but I did not run visualgo.net, so the 4 and 25 counts are unconfirmed. The current p.9 paragraph is correct as written. In CP4's own undirected reading the two arcs may be one edge, so this is a modelling difference, not a visualgo bug, and naming it is the owner's call. Ask: 'Have you seen visualgo.net give 4 bridges on CP4 4.22 and 25 on Large, Cycles, and do you want p.9 to name this difference?'

- **Issue:** The report says eager-timeline costs 'have not yet been benchmarked' (p.6) and 'Performance benchmarks … are also unavailable' (p.13). Randomized quicksort at n=10,000 took 1,328.1 ms to generate in full, with 703,821 operations and +116.94 MiB; lazily, the first operation came after 1.05 ms, with +0.49 MiB after the first batch. prototypes/src/site/ModulePage.tsx:3-8 imports GraphApp, SortingApp and TreeApp statically. That cause is an inference.

But doc 05 claim 2 only permits quoting an observation with its date, build and caveat; it does not require it. - **Issue:** The Week 10 trial is described differently from page to page. But §6.2 (p.13) says participant numbers, feedback and coded observations are not available, and §6.3 makes 'organize the Week 10 footage ... into anonymized observations linked to design changes' the first future step, so the trial-to-change link is not yet documented. The abstract carries none of the p.13 limits. p.3 calls the trial 'initial usability feedback' (§2.3) and offers it as input to the learning question (§2.1) before the trial has been introduced. 'Refinements' (plural) is never backed by any change except floating text.

No refinement other than floating text is named on any page.

- **Verifier:** Refuted as an inconsistency. Every page gives the trial the same status: formative input that informed later UI/UX work, including floating text, with no claim about learning or usability. The abstract does state that limit ('Neither source establishes…'). The link from the trial to floating text is Bao's own account of his own design decision (addendum:8, :11), disclosed on p.16. It also matches TP-34's request of 23 October to put verdicts on the canvas instead of in Status. §6.2–6.3 are about data not yet compiled, not about that motivation, so there is no need to hedge it as 'in my judgement'. Two points remain. First, the plural 'refinements' (p.1 l.39–40, p.4 l.180–181, p.13 l.583) is backed only by floating text, and only Bao knows whether the trial prompted other changes. Second, p.3 (l.118, l.151) mentions 'the Week 10 trial' before the report introduces it.

### 9.3 Risks

- **Time.** The report is due at 17:00 today. Fixes must be prioritised: the Critical items, then the factual errors, then ≤ 15 pages. A full rewrite is not realistic.
- **Usage limits.** Two workflows died on usage limits (`wf_092b4e1f-d18` and this panel's synthesis). Keep any new agent work small, or do it inline.
- **Unverifiable trial claims.** There are no dates, count, data or consent record. Overclaiming here is the biggest accuracy risk; keep the wording to what Bao confirms.
- **The private repository.** A cloud session needs access to `BJoozz/docsforfyp`. Examiners cannot open it either.
- **Uncommitted work.** The 8 report files and `docs/` are uncommitted on Bao's machine. If they are not committed and pushed, the cloud sees only `c6c371e`: the 19-page source with the hard breaks, and no handoff.
- **Shared worktrees.** OneDrive, Codex and other sessions write into the same folders. Commit by explicit path, and re-read files before editing.

### 9.4 What could not be recovered or was never available

- **The synthesis agent's output** never existed, because the agent failed.
- **The Week 10 trial material** (videos, the folder screenshot Codex was given, test questions, scores) was never seen by this session.
- **Codex's own sessions** (how it wrote the report and the 4 Nov account) are not accessible; only its commits and the message Bao pasted are.
- **Bao's answer text for Q4 "Other"** (2026-11-03) never arrived.
- **The figure capture script** of 2026-11-01 was lost; `figures/README.md` has a reconstructed replay script.
- **The full transcript** is local only, at `~/.claude/projects/c--Users-123ba-...-visualgo-plus-design/dd1448ed-3ceb-4759-a934-c360d565fa8f.jsonl` (157 MB). Every user message since 2026-11-01 was read for this handoff; the October app-development messages were only skimmed via memory.
- **The CTLT AI policy PDF** and **the registered project title** were not retrievable.

## 10. Exact Instructions for the Next Claude Session

1. **Set up.** Clone `BJoozz/docsforfyp`, which needs access to the private repo, and check that `docs/FYP_AUDIT_HANDOFF.md` is there. If `report/main.tex` line 32 still reads `\newcommand{\page}{\clearpage}`, the 8 local report changes were not pushed: ask Bao, or re-apply the macro from 2.5. Clone `kerktaiheng/visualgo-plus-design` and check out `662e7b3` for any code check. **Do not edit the app repo.**
2. **Read sections 1–9,** then load `docs/fyp-audit-panel-results.json` (structure in 7.2). The full findings with evidence are in Appendix A.
3. **Talk to Bao in Vietnamese.** Start with the questions in 9.1, giving each with its evidence (file and line, commit, PR link) and options. Bao asked for exactly that.
4. **Produce the synthesis review** (deliverable 1.3/1) from the JSON. Do not spawn a new research panel. If agents are needed, use Opus at max effort, keep them few, and make each one narrow.
5. **Before proposing any fix, reproduce the finding** in the repo. Before editing the report, get Bao's choice. Never invent trial data, quotes, counts or scores. Never re-open the settled facts in 3.3. Mention AI use at most once (the report already does, on p.5).
6. **After edits,** rebuild and check as in 7.4. Keep the English and Vietnamese versions in sync, and record what changed in `report/README.md`.
7. **Commit only on request, by explicit paths, in English,** ending with the attribution line the harness gives. Do not push unless told.

**Mistakes and rejected approaches not to repeat:**

- **Hard `\clearpage` breaks** tuned to one version of the text inflate pages after edits. Use the conditional macro or flexible floats, and re-check the page count after every edit.
- **Large agent fan-outs** died twice on usage limits. Prefer inline work or small batches near the deadline.
- **Docker for LaTeX** was rejected in favour of a direct TeX install.
- **Attribution scaffolding** (decider tags, "QUESTION who decided", trailer caveats) was rejected by Bao.
- **Never treat a Codex plan or review as Bao's agreement.**
- **Describe a screenshot only from what it shows.** One note once said F2 showed "No active run"; it actually shows BFS at frame 8/22. View PNGs before captioning.
- **Do not cite the pinned evidence snapshot `67ce56c`** for anything about the Week 10 trial; it says there was none.
- **Shell quoting in tool commands.** In this environment a doubled backslash typed into a shell command arrives single, and an apostrophe breaks single-quoted `node -e` scripts. Put scripts with backslashes or quotes in files.
- **Playwright** (if screenshots must be recaptured): pass `--output` outside OneDrive. Blank pages may be Chromium's `ERR_NETWORK_CHANGED` rather than a code bug. Do not touch port 5186 if a server is already there.

#### `floating-FT-4` (Important; CONFIRMED)

- **Where:** page 11; 4.8 timing paragraph; category technical-accuracy
- **Issue:** The timing paragraph is wrong in three places. 1. 'Notes are suppressed for intervals below 200 milliseconds': the 200 ms floor applies to the note's remaining display time, which is 90% of the step interval. A 200 ms step therefore gets no note; the first interval that shows one is about 223 ms. 2. 'Limited by the step interval and configured delay': step notes have no delay, because the writer always sets delayMs to 0. The only setting involved is Speed, which together with the step's pace gives the interval. 3. 'A manually inspected note': notes appear only when a step is reached one step forward (Next or autoplay). Previous, First, Last and the scrubber show none.  This matters for the section's own example. At the default 0.5 s speed during autoplay, a rejected Dijkstra relaxation ('· keep') note shows for about 0.3 s, and an 'already settled' note does not show at all.
- **Evidence (VERIFIED):** - prototypes/src/shared/visualization/floatingTextTiming.ts:11, :13 and :27-30: durationMs is min(1600, 0.9 × effectiveIntervalMs − delayMs) while playing and 1600 otherwise; it is undefined below 200 ms. - floatingTextTiming.test.ts:29-30: playing(223) gives 201 ms; playing(200) gives no note. - shared/visualization/stepNotes.ts:43-48: no note unless arrival.forward; delayMs is 0. useStepArrival.ts:5-10 defines 'forward'. - graph/visualization/sessionReducer.ts:5: default interval 500 ms. graph/visualization/playbackTiming.ts:15-23: pace multipliers. - graph/algorithms/dijkstra/playbackTiming.ts:20-31: an update uses 'emphasis' (1,000 ms), a keep uses 'fast' (350 ms), a settled target uses 'very-fast' (200 ms). - DD-61, docs/graph/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md:2699-2710, and the DD-17 row at line 29 say the same: 'a keep's 0.315s', and a settled target's note 'shows while playing only from 0.60 s per frame'. - The F6 screenshot footer shows 'Fast · 0.35s' on a heap compare step.
- **Proposed fix:** Replace the middle of the timing paragraph (main.tex:273) with: 'A note appears only when a step is reached one step forward (Next or autoplay); Previous, First, Last and the scrubber show none. After Next it lasts 1,600 ms; during autoplay it is cut to 90% of the step's interval (Speed × the step's pace) and omitted when less than 200 ms would remain. At the default 0.5 s speed, a Dijkstra update's note shows for 0.9 s, a kept relaxation's for about 0.3 s, and an already-settled note not at all.' To keep the length, drop 'Persistent Status text remains available, so the short-lived note is supplementary', which para 1 and the Fig. 8 caption already say. Add 'rejected steps during autoplay' to the future-work sentence.
- **Verifier:** I reproduced all three errors. - Threshold: while playing, a note lasts min(1600, 0.9 × interval − delay) ms and is dropped below 200 ms (floatingTextTiming.ts:28-29). The test at :29-30 gives 223 ms → 201 and 200 ms → none, so the cut-off is about 222 ms of step interval, not 200. - Delay: notes have none; delayMs is always 0 (stepNotes.ts:48). - Backward moves: a note shows only after a one-step-forward move (stepNotes.ts:44), so Previous, First, Last and the scrubber show none. The interval is Speed × the step's pace (useVisualizationSession.ts:105; multipliers in playbackTiming.ts:15-23; Dijkstra paces in dijkstra/playbackTiming.ts:20-31; default 500 ms in sessionReducer.ts:5). The DD-17 row (graph DD:29) and DD-61 (:2697-2710) give 0.9 s for an update, 0.315 s for a keep and nothing for a settled target at the default speed. The section's own example, a kept relaxation, is therefore the shortest-lived note in default autoplay, and a settled target gets none. Doc 02:122 states the rule correctly; the errors come from the report's paraphrase.

#### `literature-LIT-2` (Important; CONFIRMED)

- **Where:** page 3; 2 Background and Related Work (2.1-2.3); category literature-coverage
- **Issue:** Section 2 reads as a set of one-source summaries, each followed by a caveat about VisuAlgo+, not as a review organised around the report's own design questions. Its peer-reviewed base is three papers, the newest from 2012. Two of the three design questions in 2.3 (shared layout across topics; explaining steps with little visible motion) have no supporting literature, and neither has the evaluation plan in 6.3.
- **Evidence (VERIFIED):** References (p15): the peer-reviewed sources are [2] 2012, [3] 2002 and [5] 2010; [4] is a web page; [1] and [9] are library documentation; [6]-[8] are own notes. External citations appear only on pp. 2-3. Sections 4.4 (V10), 4.8 (floating text) and 6.3-6.4 cite no external source. The three design questions come only after the literature (p3, main.tex:108), and none of [2]-[5] addresses layout or on-canvas explanation. The evidence pack itself lists seven 'Topics that need external literature' (docsforfyp/05_report_mapping_and_questions.md:87-96): AV evaluation; step animation and explanations on the drawing; layout and docking; force and tidy-tree layout; test oracles; WebAssembly; accessibility. The report covers topic 1 with literature and topic 4 only with docs. The NUS SoC 'Submission Guidelines for CA Report' (comp.nus.edu.sg/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/FYP_CA_Report_Guidlines_Oct_22.pdf) require a literature review and point to the U of Toronto advice. That advice says a review must be 'organized around and related directly to the thesis or research question', is 'not just a descriptive list ... or a set of summaries', and 'It's usually a bad sign to see every paragraph beginning with the name of a researcher.' Three of the five paragraphs in 2.1-2.2 open with the source's name (main.tex:94, 98, 101). None of the caveats assesses a source's own strengths or weaknesses. For example, the report never notes that Halim et al. 2012's evidence is a self-report survey of 31 students (24 CS3233 + 7 CS2020; 'More than half ... reported enhancement ... the other half reported neutral impact', pp. 65-67 of the paper).
- **Proposed fix:** Open Section 2 with the three questions now in 2.3 and file each source under one of them. Add one limitation sentence per source, e.g. [2]'s evidence is a self-report survey of 31 students, about half 'neutral' (pp. 65-67). Q2: [2] plus Wang Baldonado et al. 2000 (doi:10.1145/345513.345271). Q3: [2]'s pseudocode request, and Chandler & Sweller 1991 (doi:10.1207/s1532690xci0804_2) for split attention. If you cite Chandler & Sweller, also report their Experiments 3-5 (abstract): 'nonessential explanatory material (e.g., a commentary on a diagram) could have deleterious effects even when presented in integrated format'. This is a redundancy risk for notes that repeat Status. Evaluation: [3] and Naps et al. 2002 (doi:10.1145/960568.782998), whose abstract says AV 'is of little educational value unless it engages learners'. For currency, add Sibia, Liut & Nobre 2025, 'Exploring the Role of Visualization Tools in Enhancing Computing Education: A Systematic Literature Review', EuroVis 2025 Education Papers, doi:10.2312/eved.20251027. DataCite confirms it; it reviews 90 papers and finds 'a lack of rigorous evaluations'. Cite only works you have read. Keep the net length unchanged: the PDF is already 16 pages against the guideline's 10-15, so recompile and check the count.
- **Verifier:** I reproduced the core claims. The peer-reviewed sources are [2] 2012, [3] 2002 and [5] 2010, and external citations appear only on pp. 2-3. The paragraphs at main.tex:94, 98 and 101 open with the source's name, and the caveats judge VisuAlgo+, never the sources. Doc 05:87-96 lists seven literature topics, and five have no source. The UofT quotes match advice.writing.utoronto.ca. New evidence settles the colleague's note (3): I rendered the CA Evaluation Form with MiKTeX pdftoppm (scratchpad/formrender/form-2.png and form-6.png). Both the supervisor's and the main evaluator's forms score 'Literature survey and review of previous works' under 'Understanding of the problem (30%)'. A-band: 'up-to-date and critical'. B-band: 'relevance ... not made clear'. C-band: 'References are outdated, lack currency'. Two corrections. First, 'no supporting literature' for Q2 overstates it: [2] backs a consistent UI across topics (abstract) and partly Q3 (students asked that 'The steps shown need to be coupled with the highlight of algorithm (pseudo)codes', Table 3, p. 66). #### `crit-problem-PU-8` (Important; CONFIRMED)

- **Where:** page 3, 11, 15; 2 Background and Related Work; References; category literature
- **Issue:** The literature is relevant but dated: the only scholarly sources are from 2002-2012, and the rest are web pages or library docs. None of it addresses the two design questions behind Bao's central contributions. Design question 3 (an explanation placed away from the drawing forces the learner to 'look away') is the well-studied split-attention / spatial-contiguity problem, yet it is argued without a source. The interaction model V10 follows (VS Code-style editor groups) is never named.
- **Evidence (VERIFIED):** References: [2] 2012, [3] 2002, [5] 2010; [4], [1] and [9] are web documentation (report-pages.txt:706-728). main.tex:265 argues 'the learner must repeatedly look away from the relevant edge' without a citation. 'VS Code' does not occur in main.tex (grep), while the V10 brief says 'Like VS Code, but with a canvas' (design/proposals/viz-shell-v10/README.md:45). On the CA form, the sub-criterion 'Literature survey and review of previous works' (top band: 'up-to-date and critical') sits under 'Understanding of the problem (30%)'. Two suggested citations, checked on the web: Chandler & Sweller (1991), Cognition and Instruction 8(4):293-332, doi:10.1207/s1532690xci0804_2; Naps et al. (2002), 'Exploring the role of visualization and engagement in computer science education', ACM SIGCSE Bulletin 35(2):131-152, doi:10.1145/960568.782998.
- **Proposed fix:** Follow the colleague's fix with two refinements, so the review is critical and not only supportive. (1) Chandler & Sweller also found that non-essential, duplicated explanation can hurt learning, and floating text repeats Status. Cite split attention as the reason for placing notes on the canvas, and name redundancy as a risk the follow-up study should check (Table 4, Dijkstra task). (2) Cite Naps et al. with one consistent year: 2003 for the SIGCSE Bulletin issue, or 2002 for the ITiCSE working-group version. Use its engagement levels to frame Table 4. In 2.3, name VS Code's editor groups as V10's design reference. Add a post-2015 source only after checking it.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced: the only scholarly sources are [3] 2002, [5] 2010 and [2] 2012; [1], [4] and [9] are web documentation (report-pages.txt:706-728). The look-away argument (main.tex:265) has no source. 'VS Code' does not occur in main.tex, although Bao's own V10 brief names it ('Like VS Code, but with a canvas', design/proposals/viz-shell-v10/README.md:45). The literature is judged twice on the Main Evaluator form: under Understanding of the problem and under 'Report - content' (ca1910.txt). Both suggested citations were checked on the web: Chandler & Sweller 1991, Cognition and Instruction 8(4):293-332, doi:10.1207/s1532690xci0804_2; Naps et al., SIGCSE Bulletin 35(2):131-152, doi:10.1145/960568.782998, dated 2002 or 2003 depending on the source.

#### `crit-technical-TA-1` (Important; CONFIRMED)

- **Where:** page 1, 3, 4, 5; Abstract; §2.3 Position of this work; §3.2; opening of §4; category contribution-framing
- **Issue:** The report never states its central technical contribution as a few positive claims that a reader can check. The abstract lists topics ('This report explains the separation of input, layout and playback state; the V10 panel-group model; ...'). §2.3 says the value 'lies in connecting them coherently' and disclaims novelty, §3.2 lists areas of work, and §4 opens with a box diagram. The hard parts and what the design guarantees are scattered across pp. 6–11, surrounded by disclaimers. Those guarantees: a run survives layout edits but not semantic edits; stale timer events are ignored; the drop preview equals the committed layout; results stay correct on multigraphs; notes are placed in screen space and timed to playback speed. An evaluator has to piece the achievement together.
- **Evidence (INFERENCE):** report-pages.txt lines 34-36 (abstract), 143-146 (§2.3), 203-208 (§3.2), 228-231 (§4.1). A grep for 'does not/cannot/not a/an/yet/evidence/claimed…' matches 31 lines. Each suggested claim below checks out at 662e7b3: prototypes/src/graph/visualization/sessionReducer.ts:36-38, 100-104, 168-171 (topology-changed ends a run; a tick counts only with the same sessionId); prototypes/src/shared/components/layout/groupsModel.test.ts:1220, 1252-1254 (4,000-step walk; the released state toStrictEqual the dropTransition result); prototypes/src/graph/algorithms/cutVertexBridge/createCutVertexBridgeSequence.ts:169-170 (parent edge skipped by id); prototypes/src/shared/visualization/stepNotePlacement.ts:1-9 and floatingTextTiming.ts:27-30; prototypes/src/sorting/comparison/operationBuffer.ts:246, 285-296; wasm-arch/engine/src/session.cc:154 ('first occurrence wins for parallel edges').
- **Proposed fix:** Add 4-6 lines at the start of §4 (p.5) answering §2.3's three questions with checkable mechanisms. Replace the abstract's 'This report explains...' with one sentence of results. Use the corrected items: (1) A run is a typed step sequence tied to the topology revision. Layout edits never end it, semantic edits do, and a tick from an older run is ignored. (2) Parallel edges and self-loops stay distinct, so cut-vertex/bridge stays correct on multigraphs. This is also the first engine-integration requirement, because the engine looks edges up by endpoints. (3) V10: one pure model and resolver for sizing, drop validity, preview and commit. A 4,000-step seeded walk checks every invariant after every step and, on each random drop, that the preview equals the released layout. (4) A shared note layer in screen space, with obstacle-aware placement and pace-aware timing, used by graph and tree. (5) One, two or four lazy sorting streams on one clock, with exact seeking by replay. (6) Six added graph algorithms checked against independent oracles; 36 tree operation ids in seven workspaces. Claim no novelty. Keep each claim's own caveat beside it (e.g. drag/split checked by hand); remove only generic repeats.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced. The abstract (lines 34-35) only lists topics. §2.3 (lines 143-151) gives a value statement and three design questions, but no section answers them in one place, so 'never' is slightly overstated. Code claims checked: sessionReducer.ts:100-104, 168-174; createCutVertexBridgeSequence.ts:169-170; session.cc:154; stepNotePlacement.ts:1-9; floatingTextTiming.ts:27-30. Four of the suggested items overclaim: (a) preview = release is asserted only on drop steps (groupsModel.test.ts:1226, 1252-1254; about 1,800 of 4,000), though invariants are checked every step (1324-1325); (b) sorting runs one, two or four panes, not 'one to four'; (c) doc 03:293 labels 'a run is immutable' as inference; (d) oracle tests cover the graph algorithms but only some tree queries (BST lower bound, select, neighbour, traversal; LCA; heap create), not all 36 ids.

#### `crit-literature-LR-1` (Important; CONFIRMED)

- **Issue:** The literature behind the project is thin and dated. [2, p.59] call 'the closest unified visualization project', plus Algorithm Visualizer and JSAV; (b) the engagement taxonomy (Naps et al. 2002) and feature-level AV studies (Saraiya et al. 2004); (c) the layout precedent the project actually followed (VS Code editor groups); (d) split-attention / spatial-contiguity research behind on-canvas explanations; (e) test-oracle / property-based testing for the §4.6 validation. The evidence pack itself planned seven literature topics. The report covers one fully (AV effectiveness) and touches one more (layout) only through library docs.

(2) Naps et al. 2002 (doi:10.1145/960568.782998). (3) Saraiya et al. 2004 (doi:10.1145/971300.971432). (4) Galles DSV (https://www.cs.usfca.edu/~galles/visualization/Algorithms.html) and/or Fouh et al. 2014 (doi:10.1016/j.scico.2013.11.040). (5) VS Code editor groups at https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/editing/getting-started/userinterface (the URL in the finding now 301-redirects there). (6) Chandler & Sweller 1992 (doi:10.1111/j.2044-8279.1992.tb01017.x). Two optional sources, only where a body sentence needs them: Reingold & Tilford 1981 (doi:10.1109/TSE.1981.234519, which d3.tree's docs name) in §4.1, and Barr et al. 2015 (doi:10.1109/TSE.2014.2372785) in §4.6. Leave out the EuroVis 2025 review: it is not in Crossref and has not been read.

Halim 2015 calls VisuAlgo the continuation of [2] and describes an instantly graded Online Quiz. visualgo.net/training is still live today. HomePage.tsx:203-204 says 'Training is not built'. There is no visualgo.net reference entry. Part (a) should be softened: visualgo.net itself calls [2] 'our 2012 paper about this system (it was not yet called VisuAlgo back in 2012)' and links the 2015 update 'to link VisuAlgo name with the previous project'. Citing [2] for VisuAlgo is therefore acceptable shorthand; the fix is to add the 2015 paper, not to call the citation wrong. One correction to the fix: 2 of the 12 playable cards are VisuAlgo+'s own editors, which visualgo.net does not have (HomePage.tsx:154). So '12 of 30 cards' does not measure coverage of VisuAlgo's catalogue.

#### `crit-literature-LR-4` (Important; CONFIRMED)

- **Where:** page 3; §2.1 ¶2; category critical-analysis
- **Issue:** The effectiveness literature is cited to draw a boundary (correctness vs learning) but is not used to justify the design, and contrary evidence is not weighed. Hundhausen et al. [3] name the learner activities that made visualizations effective, including 'constructing their own input data sets' and 'making predictions regarding future visualization states'. They report that studies in which students 'merely viewed visualizations did not demonstrate significant learning advantages', and that procedural-knowledge measures were more sensitive. Each finding bears on VisuAlgo+ (editable input, floating text as a viewing aid, the prediction tasks in Table 4), and none is used. Saraiya et al. 2004 point the other way on several VisuAlgo+ features: learner-controlled pace mattered most, a good example data set beat students' own data, and pseudocode display and stepping back showed no significant effect. A critical review should address this tension instead of treating input editing, pseudocode sync and backward seeking (§4.2) as self-evidently useful. [2]'s own student feedback is also relevant and unused: 'Visualization bugs during the early rollout affect understanding' and 'The steps shown need to be coupled with the highlight of algorithm (pseudo)codes'.
- **Evidence (VERIFIED):** Hundhausen et al. 2002 (jvlc02.pdf, pdftotext): abstract; activity list on pp.275-276; §6.2-6.3 on pp.283-284. Saraiya et al. 2004: abstract as reproduced on bibbase.org (the ACM DL page returned 403): pace control is 'the single most important feature'; a good example data set was significantly more effective than students' own; pseudocode, guiding questions and stepping back had no significant effect. Halim 2012 Table 3 (p.66). Report p.6 §4.2 ('backward stepping and seeking'); p.14 Table 4 ('Correct prediction of the next swap').
- **Proposed fix:** Replace §2.1 ¶2 with the ~120-word draft, with two changes. (1) Write 'Table 4's prediction and explanation tasks are consistent with [3]'s finding', not 'follow'. (2) Use the Naps level names (viewing, responding, changing) only after checking them in the paper; otherwise write 'engagement beyond viewing'. Keep the Saraiya caution. Keep the two Halim 2012 student comments ('Visualization bugs ... affect understanding'; steps 'coupled with the highlight of algorithm (pseudo)codes') as the link to the correctness focus and to pseudocode sync.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced in jvlc02. The activity list is on pp.275-276. 'merely viewed visualizations did not demonstrate significant learning advantages' is on p.284. The finding that procedural measures were more sensitive is on pp.281 and 283. I read Saraiya's abstract via OpenAlex: pace control is 'the single most important feature'; a good example beat students' own data sets; pseudocode display, guiding questions and backing up 'did not show a significant effect on learning'. Halim 2012's Table 3 quotes (p.66) are exact. One correction: no record says Table 4 was derived from [3], so writing 'follow' would invent a rationale. The Naps level names are still unchecked; the abstract confirms only 'a new taxonomy of learner engagement'.

#### `crit-literature-LR-6` (Important; CONFIRMED)

- **Where:** page 7, 11 (and 3); §4.4 V10 layout; §4.8 Floating text; §2.3; category design-justification
- **Issue:** Two flagship design choices are justified without the precedents the project actually used. The project records say V10's interaction follows VS Code: drag feedback, splitter behaviour, edge-band drops and per-panel scrolling. The report never says so: §2.3 only says dockable panels are 'not claimed as new', and §4.4 cites only [6]. It also does not mention that general docking libraries for React exist (e.g. dockview, Golden Layout), or why a custom pure model was built instead. Floating text (§4.8) is justified by attention switching and by 'Prof. Halim's canvas-focused educational goal, as recalled', but not by the split-attention and spatial-contiguity research on this exact problem. Citing that research would make the rationale independent of recollection. A smaller uncited influence: V1's README credits 'CSAcademy-inspired visuals'.
- **Evidence (DOCUMENTED):** PR #15 body (gh pr view 15, merged 2026-10-17): 'Drag feedback follows VS Code: the frame shows the area the tab will take, named, with a ghost under the pointer; an empty region opens from an edge band of the drawing'. design/proposals/viz-shell-v10/README.md at 662e7b3, lines 45, 56, 61, 64-67 and 78: choices 11, 16, 19-22 and 33 cite VS Code. The same proposal names no library alternative (grep for library, dockview, golden, flexlayout and mosaic: 0 hits). TP-34 (prototypes/docs/tree/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md:2683-2702): the owner's request of 2026-10-23 for text 'right on the canvas instead of showing too much in Status, especially the dead steps'. V1 README.md:26: 'CSAcademy-inspired visuals'. Checked the VS Code 'Editor groups' docs, dockview.dev ('The docking layout manager for React') and github.com/golden-layout/golden-layout ('A multi window layout manager for webapps').
- **Proposed fix:** In §4.4 ¶1, add: 'The interaction follows the editor groups of Visual Studio Code [VSC]: a tab can join another group or split one from its edge. V10 adds a canvas that is never a tab and required panels that always remain.' Use https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/editing/getting-started/userinterface. In §4.8, after 'look away from the relevant edge', add: 'This is the split-attention problem described by Chandler and Sweller [CS92].' (doi:10.1111/j.2044-8279.1992.tb01017.x). In §2.3, cite the precedent instead of the bare disclaimer. Add a sentence on not using a docking library only if the owner gives a real reason. Ask: was dockview or Golden Layout considered, and why build a custom model?
- **Owner must confirm:** Why was V10 built as a custom pure layout model instead of on a docking library such as dockview or Golden Layout? Was any library tried?
- **Verifier:** Reproduced. The PR #15 body (merged 2026-10-17) says 'Drag feedback follows VS Code'. design/proposals/viz-shell-v10/README.md:45 gives the brief 'Like VS Code, but with a canvas', and choices 11, 16, 19-22 and 33 name VS Code; choice 11 is in the owner's own words. No docking library is named or installed (git grep at 662e7b3: 0 hits; none in prototypes/package.json). TP-34 at DESIGN_DECISIONS.md:2683-2702 checks out. VS Code's docs describe editor groups and moving tabs between them. This is the same gap as LR-1 (c) and (d), so count it once. Two small corrections: the VS Code URL now 301-redirects, and I could not open Mayer & Moreno 2003, so its 'extraneous load' wording should not be used unchecked. The CSAcademy point is low value.

#### `crit-accuracy-ACC-6` (Important; CONFIRMED)

- **Where:** page 12; 5; category coverage-claim
- **Issue:** 'Of the 30 catalogue cards, 12 open a module; eight are labelled full and four partial' mixes two kinds of card and leaves 'full' undefined. Of the 26 cards taken from visualgo.net's module list, only four are full (Sorting, BST/AVL, Binary Heap, MST) and four partial (Segment Tree, Graph Traversal, SSSP, Max Flow). The other four 'full' cards are Trie and LCA, which are visualgo.net pages it does not list, and this project's own Graph and Tree Editors, where 'full' means 'every one it lists'. 'Full' also counts algorithm names, not operations. The evidence pack asked for the definition to be given with every count. A reader can take 'eight full' to mean eight VisuAlgo modules rebuilt.
- **Evidence (VERIFIED):** visualgo-plus-design/prototypes/src/site/modules.ts:3-18 (26 transcribed cards; 'Trie and LCA are pages visualgo.net has but does not list; the Graph Editor and Tree Editor are VisuAlgo+'s own'), :21-27 (the 'full' definition), :157-359 (statuses: 8 full, 4 partial); docsforfyp/05_report_mapping_and_questions.md:116, :216 (row 14: give the counts with the definition of 'full').
- **Proposed fix:** 'Of the 30 catalogue cards, 12 open a module and 18 are marked coming soon. Of the 26 cards transcribed from visualgo.net, eight open: four offer every algorithm the original module lists (Sorting, BST/AVL, Binary Heap, MST) and four offer some of them (Segment Tree, Graph Traversal, SSSP, Max Flow). The other four open cards are Trie and LCA, which visualgo.net has as unlisted pages, and this project's Graph and Tree Editors. "Full" counts algorithms, not operations [7].'
- **Verifier:** Reproduced. modules.ts:21-27 defines 'full' as every algorithm the real module has or, for an editor, every algorithm it lists. The statuses (:157-362) give eight full cards (Graph Editor, Tree Editor, Sorting, BST/AVL, Binary Heap, MST, Trie, LCA) and four partial ones (Segment Tree, Graph Traversal, SSSP, Max Flow). Only four of the 26 cards transcribed from visualgo.net are full, and two of the eight 'full' cards are this project's own editors. The report never defines 'full', although the pack's own rule asks for the definition with every count (05:216, row 14). The supervisor wrote the original modules, so 'eight full' invites a wrong count; the existing 'not complete operation parity' caveat does not fix this.
